+# Rally Detection Code Review
+
+## 1. How Rally Detection Works
+
+The project identifies rallies through a combination of local trajectory tracking (e.g., TrackNet or WASB) and a series of heuristic gating/trimming rules, culminating in an AI-based refinement (Phase 3). Here's a breakdown of the pipeline:
+
+### Phase 2: Candidate Windowing (The Heuristics)
+1. **Velocity Thresholding:** The trajectory detector outputs `(Frame, Visibility, X, Y)`. For each visible frame, the normalized velocity (distance divided by frame width per second) is calculated. Frames with a velocity exceeding `velocity_thresh` (e.g., 0.02) are considered "active" (meaning the shuttle is in flight).
+2. **Merging / Grouping:** Active frames within a certain dead time (`merge_gap`, usually 2.0s) are merged into candidate windows.
+3. **Splitting Over-merged Windows (W1):** If a window exceeds `max_window_duration`, the code splits it at the largest internal gap (dead-time) that is at least `min_split_gap`. If the window lacks gaps (the shuttle never stops moving), a `window_hard_cap` setting (if enabled) forcibly chops it in half at the temporal midpoint.
+4. **Inactive Tail Trimming (R4):** Even after a rally finishes, a player might pick up the shuttle and toss it, creating a slow-moving trajectory tail. To prevent the window from overextending, the algorithm trims the end back to the last segment of the window that had a dense cluster of "fast" frames (velocity > `rally_thresh`).
+5. **Blob Fallback (R5):** If a window still vastly exceeds the cap after the W1 split and R4 trim (meaning it's an unusually continuous blob of motion), it is chopped down to the maximum cap limit and marked with a `forced_cut` flag. This signals that the clip needs "rally-state" cues (like net-crossings) rather than a simple duration cap.
+6. **Rally Gate (Gate A):** In `rally_gate.py`, a heuristic filters out "false" rallies (like a player tossing a shuttle back to their opponent). It estimates the net position (median play axis coordinate) and counts how many times the shuttle crosses the net. A valid rally must have `min_crossings` (e.g., 2).
+
+### Phase 3: AI Handoff
+Valid candidate windows are padded (e.g., by 2 seconds on each end) and sent to a Generative AI Provider (like Gemini) along with a sports-specific prompt. The AI confirms the semantic boundaries, ensuring the window correctly matches a true rally, and also attempts to count the shuttle exchanges.
+
+---
+
+## 2. Deep Code Review & Potential Issues Found
+
+I performed a deep review of `tracknet_runner.py`, `trajectory_hybrid.py`, `rally_gate.py`, and the parsing logic in `sports-data-collector`. Here are the findings and hidden issues:
+
+### A. Subtle Calculation Artifact in `_spread` (`rally_gate.py`)
+**Observation:** The `_spread` function calculates a robust spread (ignoring the bottom 5% and top 5% of coordinates to eliminate outliers):
+`lo = s[max(0, int(0.05 * (len(s) - 1)))]`
+`hi = s[min(len(s) - 1, int(0.95 * (len(s) - 1)))]`
+
+**Hidden Behavior:** Because `int()` truncates rather than rounds, for very short arrays (e.g., `< 20` items), the 95th percentile will simply be the second-to-last item, and for `len(s) == 2`, the spread evaluates to strictly `0.0`. 
+
+**Impact:** This doesn't crash the pipeline, because the play axis is correctly estimated over the *entire* video's thousands of trajectory points. However, if this utility function is reused on small window slices in the future, it will quietly collapse the spread to zero.
+
+### B. Sensitivity to Sparse Tracking in `_trim_inactive_tail` (`tracknet_runner.py`)
+**Observation:** The inactivity trimmer identifies the end of a rally by looking for the last frame where the trailing `trail_frames` window has a sufficient density ...

48	    if not vals:
49	        return 0.0
50	    s = sorted(vals)

55	def estimate_play_axis(points) -> Tuple[str, float, float]:
56	    """Return (axis 'x'|'y', net_value, span) from visible points.
57	
58	    Play axis = the image axis along which the shuttle travels most (larger spread).
59	    For a baseline camera that's Y (players top/bottom); for a side camera, X.
60	    """
61	    xs = [p.x for p in points if p.visible]
62	    ys = [p.y for p in points if p.visible]
63	    sx, sy = _spread(xs), _spread(ys)
64	    if sx >= sy:
65	        return "x", _median(xs), sx
66	    return "y", _median(ys), sy
67

69	def count_net_crossings(window_points, axis: str, net: float, deadband: float) -> int:
70	    """Count sign changes of (coord - net) across visible points, ignoring the
71	    deadband zone near the net (so jitter at the net doesn't inflate the count)."""
72	    crossings = 0
73	    last_side = None
74	    for p in window_points:
75	        if not p.visible:
76	            continue
77	        v = (p.x if axis == "x" else p.y) - net
78	        if abs(v) < deadband:
79	            continue
80	        side = v > 0
81	        if last_side is not None and side != last_side:
82	            crossings += 1
83	        last_side = side
84	    return crossings
85

402	    def _trim_inactive_tail(group: List[Tuple[int, float]], trail_frames: int,
403	                            min_density: float, rally_thresh: float) -> List[Tuple[int, float]]:
404	        """R4: trim the post-rally drift tail. After a rally the shuttle keeps moving *slowly*
405	        (picked up / walked / knock-up) above ``velocity_thresh``, so the velocity windowing
406	        over-extends the END. A frame is "fast" (real rally motion) iff its velocity > ``rally_thresh``
407	        (higher than the active threshold). The rally is "on" at frame ``i`` while its trailing
408	        ``trail_frames`` window holds >= ``min_density`` fraction of fast frames; trim back to the
409	        LAST such frame — everything after is sustained slow drift (rally over). Only cuts (recall
410	        can't rise); a window whose tail never goes slow is untouched, and if no fast-dense point
411	        exists at all the group is kept whole (fail-safe, no over-trim). O(n) two-pointer; the START
412	        is left alone (already ~Gemini-accurate per the n=2 boundary-error finding)."""
413	        if trail_frames <= 0 or len(group) < 2:

420	        for i in range(len(frames)):

422	            while frames[lo] <= frames[i] - trail_frames:
423	                fast_sum -= fast[lo]
424	                lo += 1
425	            win_count = i - lo + 1
426	            if win_count > 0 and fast_sum / win_count >= min_density:
427	                last_dense = i
428	        return group[: last_dense + 1] if last_dense >= 0 else group

91	def gate_windows(points, windows: List[Interval], fps: float,
92	                 min_crossings: int = 2, deadband_frac: float = 0.06,
93	                 estimate: Optional[Tuple[str, float, float]] = None) -> List[GatedWindow]:
94	    """Annotate each (start,end) window with its shuttle net-crossing count and a
95	    keep decision (crossings >= min_crossings). Deadband is a fraction of the play
96	    axis span. Net/axis are estimated once over the whole trajectory (stable).
97	
98	    `estimate` lets a caller scoring MANY windows off one trajectory pass a precomputed
99	    `(axis, net, span)` (from `estimate_play_axis`) so the O(P log P) estimate isn't redone
100	    per window — same result, avoids the per-window re-estimation."""
101	    axis, net, span = estimate if estimate is not None else estimate_play_axis(points)
102	    deadband = deadband_frac * span
103	    out: List[GatedWindow] = []
104	    for (s, e) in windows:
105	        wp = _points_in(points, fps, s, e)
106	        c = count_net_crossings(wp, axis, net, deadband)
107	        vis = sum(1 for p in wp if p.visible)
108	        out.append(GatedWindow(start=s, end=e, crossings=c, visible_points=vis,
109	                               kept=c >= min_crossings))
110	    return out
111	
112	
113	def apply_gate(points, windows: List[Interval], fps: float,
114	               min_crossings: int = 2, deadband_frac: float = 0.06) -> List[Interval]:
115	    """Convenience: return only the windows that pass the crossing gate."""
116	    return [(g.start, g.end) for g in
117	            gate_windows(points, windows, fps, min_crossings, deadband_frac) if g.kept]

## My recommendation: **don't merge, and don't "fund" anything**

There's no project to fund here. The PR body's own words are *"once approved, I will implement the code changes"* — no resourcing, budget, or headcount ask anywhere. What's actually proposed is a **~3-spot localized cleanup**: two functions in this repo and one log line in the sibling repo. The "merge and fund the project" framing is badly oversized relative to the content. I'd drop that lens entirely and treat it as ordinary review.

I reproduced every claim against the real code (not the write-up) via parallel adversarial verification. The three proposed fixes split cleanly:

| | Claim | Reality | What to do |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | `_spread` "divide-by-zero" / zero-spread | **Non-bug.** `span` is only ever *multiplied* (`deadband = 0.06 * span`, [rally_gate.py:102](backend/pipeline/detectors/rally_gate.py:102)) — there is no division anywhere. It's only called over the whole video's trajectory, never small slices, and already returns `0.0` for `len<2` *by design*. The `int()→ceil()` "fix" would silently shift the play-axis span/deadband on **every** video for zero benefit. | **Drop it** |
| **B** | `_trim_inactive_tail` sparse-tail density | **Real but low-severity** edge case (reproduced: a ~2.0s rally over-extends to 3.87s). But this *is* R4 — the **default-ON, measured, tuned** rally-END trimmer ([models.py:150-155](backend/config/models.py:150): ΔtolF1 +0.040, p=0.022, n=13) — and rally-END is the project's **#1 quality gap**. The proposed denominator swap, with `min_density` untouched, **regresses normal rallies by ~6s** in simulation. | **Don't blind-patch.** Route into the gap-closure track with a Launch Report + dual-set regression |
| **C** | `rally_cvat.py` silent row-skip | **Accurate, genuinely useful** observability nit (a `logger.warning`). | Valid — but it's in **sports-data-collector**, so it can't ride this PR. Land it there |
| **D** | W1/R5 splitter termination praise | **Correct** — the `len<2` guard + `_midpoint_split_index ∈ [1,len-1]` genuinely guarantee termination. | (no action) |

**The one thing that needs your eye:** fix B isn't junk — the contributor correctly found a real quirk, and it happens to touch *exactly* your top lever (the R4 end-rule). That's the trap: precisely because R4 is default-ON and measured, this can't be a "harden the edge case, tests pass" code change — it would invalidate the tuned `min_density=0.30` calibration. It belongs in the owner-gated rally-END effort with measurement, not this PR.

Net: the contributor's code-reading is real and welcome (D is right, the pipeline narrative is mostly accurate, B is a legitimate catch). The problem is **calibration** — a non-bug escalated to "divide-by-zero," and a measured production lever treated as a harmless tweak.

**Bottom line:** request changes → drop A, re-home C to the collector repo, promote B into the measured rally-END track. I posted exactly this on the PR.

If you'd like, I can: (a) open the C fix as its own PR in `sports-data-collector`, and/or (b) draft a proper measured plan for the B / sparse-tail end-rule under `RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md`. Want me to do either?

Also worth a quick cleanup regardless of this PR: the in-code comment at [tracknet_runner.py:374](backend/pipeline/detectors/tracknet_runner.py:374) still says R4 is "default OFF," but config has it ON at 1.5 — stale and misleading.

1	# Rally Detection Code Review
2	
3	## 1. How Rally Detection Works
4	
5	The project identifies rallies through a combination of local trajectory tracking (e.g., TrackNet or WASB) and a series of heuristic gating/trimming rules, culminating in an AI-based refinement (Phase 3). Here's a breakdown of the pipeline:
6	
7	### Phase 2: Candidate Windowing (The Heuristics)
8	1. **Velocity Thresholding:** The trajectory detector outputs `(Frame, Visibility, X, Y)`. For each visible frame, the normalized velocity (distance divided by frame width per second) is calculated. Frames with a velocity exceeding `velocity_thresh` (e.g., 0.02) are considered "active" (meaning the shuttle is in flight).
9	2. **Merging / Grouping:** Active frames within a certain dead time (`merge_gap`, usually 2.0s) are merged into candidate windows.
10	3. **Splitting Over-merged Windows (W1):** If a window exceeds `max_window_duration`, the code splits it at the largest internal gap (dead-time) that is at least `min_split_gap`. If the window lacks gaps (the shuttle never stops moving), a `window_hard_cap` setting (if enabled) forcibly chops it in half at the temporal midpoint.
11	4. **Inactive Tail Trimming (R4):** Even after a rally finishes, a player might pick up the shuttle and toss it, creating a slow-moving trajectory tail. To prevent the window from overextending, the algorithm trims the end back to the last segment of the window that had a dense cluster of "fast" frames (velocity > `rally_thresh`).
12	5. **Blob Fallback (R5):** If a window still vastly exceeds the cap after the W1 split and R4 trim (meaning it's an unusually continuous blob of motion), it is chopped down to the maximum cap limit and marked with a `forced_cut` flag. This signals that the clip needs "rally-state" cues (like net-crossings) rather than a simple duration cap.
13	6. **Rally Gate (Gate A):** In `rally_gate.py`, a heuristic filters out "false" rallies (like a player tossing a shuttle back to their opponent). It estimates the net position (median play axis coordinate) and counts how many times the shuttle crosses the net. A valid rally must have `min_crossings` (e.g., 2).
14	
15	### Phase 3: AI Handoff
16	Valid candidate windows are padded (e.g., by 2 seconds on each end) and sent to a Generative AI Provider (like Gemini) along with a sports-specific prompt. The AI confirms the semantic boundaries, ensuring the window correctly matches a true rally, and also attempts to count the shuttle exchanges.
17	
18	---
19	
20	## 2. Deep Code Review & Potential Issues Found
21	
22	I performed a deep review of `tracknet_runner.py`, `trajectory_hybrid.py`, `rally_gate.py`, and the parsing logic in `sports-data-collector`. Here are the findings and hidden issues:
23	
24	### A. Subtle Calculation Artifact in `_spread` (`rally_gate.py`)
25	**Observation:** The `_spread` function calculates a robust spread (ignoring the bottom 5% and top 5% of coordinates to eliminate outliers):
26	`lo = s[max(0, int(0.05 * (len(s) - 1)))]`
27	`hi = s[min(len(s) - 1, int(0.95 * (len(s) - 1)))]`
28	
29	**Hidden Behavior:** Because `int()` truncates rather than rounds, for very short arrays (e.g., `< 20` items), the 95th percentile will simply be the second-to-last item, and for `len(s) == 2`, the spread evaluates to strictly `0.0`. 
30	
31	**Impact:** This doesn't crash the pipeline, because the play axis is correctly estimated over the *entire* video's thousands of trajectory points. However, if this utility function is reused on small window slices in the future, it will quietly collapse the spread to zero.
32	
33	### B. Sensitivity to Sparse Tracking in `_trim_inactive_tail` (`tracknet_runner.py`)
34	**Observation:** The inactivity trimmer identifies the end of a rally by looking for the last frame where the trailing `trail_frames` window has a sufficient density of fast-moving frames (`fast_sum / win_count >= min_density`).
35	
36	**Hidden Issue:** `win_count` represents the number of *visible, active fra...

4	
5	# Refactor Rally Detection Edge Cases & Parser Stability
6	
7	> **Status:** `IN PROGRESS` · **Owner:** `Agent` · **Created:** `2026-06-19` · **Last updated:** `2026-06-19`
8	> **Lifecycle:** `DRAFT → IN PROGRESS → DONE → archived`
9	> **Tracking anchors:** §7 progress tracker is the source of truth; indexed in `docs/README.md`.
10	> **Relation to existing docs:** Extends `RALLY_DETECTION_QUALITY_REPORT.md`.
11	
12	---
13	
14	## 0. TL;DR
15	The rally detection pipeline is largely robust, but a deep code review highlighted three minor edge cases/bugs: a potential divide-by-zero or zero-spread in `_spread` for tiny sequences, a density calculation vulnerability for sparse tracks in `_trim_inactive_tail`, and silent skipping of malformed rows in the CVAT parsing script. This plan outlines the fixes for these issues to increase stability.
16	
17	## 1. Problem & goal
18	The current implementation of the candidate windowing handles normal inputs extremely well (including handling blobs), but could fail or behave erratically under specific conditions:
19	- **Zero-spread calculation**: `_spread` in `rally_gate.py` evaluates to `0.0` for arrays of length 2, which could cause a zero deadband if used on short clips.
20	- **Sparse trailing frames**: `_trim_inactive_tail` calculates frame density based on *visible* frames. A long gap followed by a single active frame gives 100% density, artificially inflating the rally end.
21	- **Silent failure on malformed CSV**: `load_canonical_csv` in `rally_cvat.py` silently ignores rows without a valid `rally_number`.
22	
23	**Goal**: Fix these three issues to harden the rally detection and ingestion pipelines without changing the existing heuristic thresholds. 
24	
25	## 4. Design / architecture
26	The changes will be entirely localized to the identified functions:
27	- `rally_gate.py`: Modify `_spread` to use `math.ceil()` or properly enforce a minimum subset size before trimming percentiles, ensuring it never inadvertently calculates a spread of 0 for non-identical values.
28	- `tracknet_runner.py`: Update `_trim_inactive_tail` to factor the *temporal length* (or total expected frames in the trailing window) into the density calculation, rather than purely relying on `win_count` (the number of visible frames). 
29	- `rally_cvat.py`: Introduce `logger.warning` before the `continue` statement in `load_canonical_csv` to surface skipped rows during CVAT ingestion.
30	
31	## 6. Honest limits — what this does NOT do
32	This will NOT change the core AI validation (Phase 3) or the existing `velocity_thresh` values. It solely tightens up boundary calculation edge cases. It will not suddenly "fix" rallies that the tracking model missed entirely due to lighting or occlusion.
33	
34	## 7. Deliverables & progress tracker   ⟵ **source of truth**
35	Legend: ☐ Todo · ◐ In progress · ☑ Done · ⛔ Blocked/gated. **One small PR per row**, branched from `origin/master`, no stacking. Update the row (status + PR link) as work lands.

38	|----|-------------|-----------|--------|--------|----|
39	| P0 | Fix `_spread` and `_trim_inactive_tail` edge cases | — | No | ☐ | — |
40	| P1 | Add logging to `load_canonical_csv` for silent skips | — | No | ☐ | — |
41	
42	## 8. Open questions — owner / external
43	1. Do we want `_trim_inactive_tail` to fall back to the existing behavior if there are very few frames, or strictly enforce a minimum temporal density? (Assuming strict temporal density is preferred for accuracy).
44

### 110. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.253Z)

Done. Here's the answer to both parts of your question.

## Why it was so complex
It wasn't really complexity — it was **unbounded append-only growth**. `current-state.md` is meant to be the file you read *first, every session*, but the checkpoint convention says "flush a checkpoint at every micro-checkpoint" and **never said to prune**. So 98 full session checkpoints (2026-06-06 → 06-19) piled up in one file: **2,376 lines / 315 KB** — so big it couldn't even be opened in a single read. Only the top ~30 lines were ever "live"; the other ~2,340 were history that should have been archived long ago.

## What I did — sharded it (nothing lost)
| File | Before | After | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| `current-state.md` | 2,376 lines / 315 KB | **39 lines / 17 KB** | **Live only** — current handoff + today's 2 checkpoints + the prune rule + a pointer |
| `current-state-archive.md` | — | 2,355 lines | **Full history**, verbatim, newest-first (96 aged-out checkpoints) |

Integrity checked: **2 live + 96 archived = 98** checkpoints, the archive ends cleanly at the oldest 2026-06-06 content, and no backup/temp files left behind.

I also closed the loop so it **can't regrow**:
- Added a **scope banner** at the top of the live file: live = handoff + *today's* checkpoints only.
- Added the **prune rule** to the convention that caused this ([working-agreement-checkpoints.md](../memory/working-agreement-checkpoints.md)): when checkpoints age out (new clean-slate day), move them to the archive instead of stacking — checkpoints are still flushed every micro-checkpoint, just relocated once stale.
- Updated [MEMORY.md](../memory/MEMORY.md) "Start here" so the index points at both files and marks the archive as deep-history-only.

Net effect: the read-first file is now an actual ~1-pager, and the detailed measured history (which you value for the paper aim) is fully preserved, just out of the hot path.

One thing I deliberately **didn't** do: `session-handoff.md` overlaps with the live handoff block here, so there's still some duplication between the two "live" files. I left that alone since you asked specifically about `current-state.md` — but if you want, I can reconcile them so the handoff block points at `session-handoff.md` instead of repeating it. Want me to?

########## docs/archives/README.md ##########
# archives/

Umbrella directory for all **historical and completed** material.

## Live vs Historical
- **Live docs** (for ramp-up, constant updates, and current work) live at the top level of `docs/`.
- Everything here is no longer the primary source of truth. It is kept for context, decision history, and regression detection.

## Classification inside archives/
- `past_projects/` — Completed small tech debt, starter projects, or low-regret initiatives (e.g. the original low-regret moves + scaffolding work). These are "past" once they reach a terminal state.
- `decisions/` — Historical ADRs and major architectural decisions.
- `research/` — Completed or superseded research notes and plans.
- `checkpoints/` — Snapshots of major project states (especially useful for detecting regressions in quality, priorities, or baselines over time as we do quality iterations).
- Other one-off audits, etc.

## Archival Rule
Only move something into `archives/` (or its subdirectories) when the work is in a clear **terminal state**:
- DONE
- COMPLETED
- REJECTED

Never archive in-flight or pending work.

When archiving a project/phase:
1. Ensure the main document has an explicit `**STATUS: COMPLETED**` (or DONE/REJECTED) header at the top.
2. Link back to the current living documents in `../` (e.g. `COMMERCIALIZATION.md`, `BACKLOG.md`).
3. For regression detection value, consider adding a dated entry under `checkpoints/`.

See `past_projects/README.md` for the specific guidance on that category.

########## docs/archives/past_projects/README.md ##########
# past_projects/

This subdirectory is for **completed small tech debt, starter projects, or low-regret initiatives**.

## Criteria for past_projects/
- The work was a bounded "project" (e.g. low-regret moves, scaffolding maturation, specific tech debt cleanup).
- It has reached a terminal state (STATUS: DONE / COMPLETED / REJECTED).
- It is no longer the active focus (new work happens via the live docs + BACKLOG).

## Example
- `low-regret-moves/LOW_REGRET_MOVES_PLAN.md` — the 2026 low-regret + scaffolding phase. Marked COMPLETED after the three scaffolding PRs were merged.

Future small completed starter projects or tech debt cleanups that are similar in nature should land here (with their own subdir and clear STATUS header).

Larger ongoing quality iterations or major feature work will typically use dated entries under `../checkpoints/` or `../quality-iterations/` instead.

1	# KhelSutra — Product Overview & Early-User Guide
2	
3	*What it does today, how to get the best out of it, and where it's going.*
4	*For field associates and for invited alpha / beta academies, coaches, and players.*
5	
6	---
7	
8	## In one line
9	
10	**KhelSutra turns ordinary badminton video into useful pieces — automatically.** Put a phone or GoPro
11	on a tripod behind the court, record a session, and KhelSutra automatically finds the rallies, stitches
12	a **highlight reel**, and gives you a **simple summary** (how many rallies, how much real play time).
13	No special hardware, no editing, no manual scrubbing.
14	
15	---
16	
17	## The problem we're solving
18	
19	Academies and serious players already record a lot of video — and then almost never watch it. Hours of
20	footage pile up on phones and memory cards because cutting it into useful clips by hand is tedious and
21	nobody has the time. The good rallies, the moments worth reviewing, stay buried.
22	
23	Most academy halls also run **several courts at once**, so a single recording is a busy, noisy scene
24	that ordinary video tools choke on. **Pulling the real rallies out of busy, multi-court footage is the
25	hard problem KhelSutra is built to crack** — it already works very well on clean, single-court
26	recordings, and the multi-court case is exactly what we're sharpening now.
27	
28	---
29	
30	## Who it's for
31	
32	- **Coaching academies & coaches** — turn raw session footage into review-ready clips and reels for
33	  teaching, without an editor.
34	- **Serious players** — get clips of your own rallies to review, keep, and share.
35	- Today's focus is **badminton academies in Bengaluru** — that's who we're building with first.
36	
37	---
38	
39	## What it does **today**
40	
41	This is what's working right now, in the private web app invited users can try:
42	
43	- **Upload a full session or match video** (from a phone on a tripod, or a GoPro). Long videos are fine.
44	- **Automatic rally detection** — it finds the rallies and where they start and end, on its own.
45	- **Highlight reel** — the rallies stitched into one MP4 you can watch, download, and share, with a small
46	  *"Generated by Khelsutra.guru"* mark.
47	- **A simple summary** — how many rallies were found and how much actual play time there was.
48	- **Re-use & download** — process once, come back to it later, download the reel.
49	
50	That's the core loop: **record → upload → get back a reel + summary.** No editing skill needed.
51	
52	> Like any automatic tool, it can occasionally miss a real rally or include one that isn't — telling us
53	> when that happens is genuinely useful feedback, not a sign it's broken (more on that below).
54	
55	---
56	
57	## How to use it (alpha)
58	
59	1. **Your KhelSutra associate helps you get access** to the private web app (it's invite-only; today the
60	   app is in English).
61	2. **Upload your session video.** Large files upload in pieces and can take a while on Indian uplinks —
62	   that's normal.
63	3. **It processes in the background.** You can close the tab and come back later; you don't have to sit
64	   and watch it.
65	4. **When it's done**, you get the **highlight reel + summary** to watch and download.
66	5. **Re-process anytime.** (Re-running the same video replaces its earlier result rather than keeping both.)
67	
68	---
69	
70	## How to get the best out of it (capture matters)
71	
72	KhelSutra works best on a **steady, full-court recording** — the same way a coach would set a camera to
73	review play. A few simple habits make a big difference to the results:
74	
75	- **Put the camera behind the court, slightly elevated**, with the **whole court in frame**.
76	- **Keep it still** — a fixed tripod beats a handheld or panning shot every time.
77	- **Shoot landscape**, with reasonable lighting.
78	- **Record the whole session** and upload it as-is — KhelSutra does the cutting.
79	
80	Footage that fights these habits (shaky handheld, heavy zooming/pan...

### 143. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.255Z)

# Imagine — Visual Creation Suite

## Modules
Call read_me again with the modules parameter to load detailed guidance:
- `diagram` — SVG flowcharts, structural diagrams, illustrative diagrams
- `mockup` — UI mockups, forms, cards, dashboards
- `interactive` — interactive explainers with controls
- `chart` — charts, data analysis, geographic maps (Chart.js, D3 choropleth)
- `art` — illustration and generative art
Pick the closest fit. The module includes all relevant design guidance.

**Complexity budget — hard limits:**
- Box subtitles: ≤5 words. Detail goes in click-through (`sendPrompt`) or the prose below — not the box.
- Colors: ≤2 ramps per diagram. If colors encode meaning (states, tiers), add a 1-line legend. Otherwise use one neutral ramp.
- Horizontal tier: ≤4 boxes at full width (~140px each). 5+ boxes → shrink to ≤110px OR wrap to 2 rows OR split into overview + detail diagrams.

If you catch yourself writing "click to learn more" in prose, the diagram itself must ACTUALLY be sparse. Don't promise brevity then front-load everything.

**Accessibility:** For HTML widgets, begin with a visually-hidden `<h2 class="sr-only">` containing a one-sentence summary of the visualization for screen-reader users. (SVG widgets use `role="img"` with `<title>` and `<desc>` instead — see SVG setup.)

You create rich visual content — SVG diagrams/illustrations and HTML interactive widgets — that renders inline in conversation. The best output feels like a natural extension of the chat.

## Core Design System

These rules apply to ALL use cases.

### Philosophy
- **Seamless**: Users shouldn't notice where claude.ai ends and your widget begins.
- **Flat**: No gradients, mesh backgrounds, noise textures, or decorative effects. Clean flat surfaces.
- **Compact**: Show the essential inline. Explain the rest in text.
- **Text goes in your response, visuals go in the tool** — All explanatory text, descriptions, introductions, and summaries must be written as normal response text OUTSIDE the tool call. The tool output should contain ONLY the visual element (diagram, chart, interactive widget). Never put paragraphs of explanation, section headings, or descriptive prose inside the HTML/SVG. If the user asks "explain X", write the explanation in your response and use the tool only for the visual that accompanies it. The user's font settings only apply to your response text, not to text inside the widget.

### Streaming
Output streams token-by-token. Structure code so useful content appears early.
- **HTML**: `<style>` (short) → content HTML → `<script>` last.
- **SVG**: `<defs>` (markers) → visual elements immediately.
- Prefer inline `style="..."` over `<style>` blocks — inputs/controls must look correct mid-stream.
- Keep `<style>` under ~15 lines. Interactive widgets with inputs and sliders need more style rules — that's fine, but don't bloat with decorative CSS.
- Gradients, shadows, and blur flash during streaming DOM diffs. Use solid flat fills instead.

### Rules
- No `<!-- comments -->` or `/* comments */` (waste tokens, break streaming)
- No font-size below 11px
- No emoji. Icons = Tabler **outline** webfont (5800+, already loaded): `<i class="ti ti-home"></i>`. Outline only — never use `-filled` suffixes (`ti-heart-filled` etc. are not loaded and will render blank). Inherits color + font-size from parent. Decorative icons get `aria-hidden="true"`; icon-only buttons get `aria-label`. Common: ti-home ti-settings ti-user ti-search ti-x ti-check ti-plus ti-trash ti-edit ti-download ti-upload ti-file ti-folder ti-chart-bar ti-calendar ti-clock ti-arrow-right ti-arrow-left ti-chevron-down ti-external-link ti-copy ti-refresh ti-player-play ti-player-pause ti-heart ti-star ti-bell ti-mail ti-lock ti-eye ti-menu-2. Don't hand-draw icon SVG paths.

## Ship it to khelsutra.guru on Cloudflare Pages

**1 · Deploy the page (free, no git needed)**
- Cloudflare dashboard → **Workers & Pages → Create → Pages → Upload assets**.
- Name it `khelsutra-site`, drag in the `landing/` folder (the one holding `index.html`), Deploy → you get a live `khelsutra-site.pages.dev` URL instantly.

**2 · Point the domain at it**
- Add `khelsutra.guru` to Cloudflare (dashboard → **Add a site**). It gives you **2 nameservers** → set those at the registrar where you bought it. (This hands DNS to Cloudflare and makes the next steps one-click.)
- In the Pages project → **Custom domains → Set up a domain** → `khelsutra.guru` (and `www`). Cloudflare auto-creates the records and provisions **free HTTPS**.

**3 · Make the CTA actually receive mail** (since the CTA is email)
- Domain → **Email → Email Routing → Enable** → add address `hello@khelsutra.guru` → forward to `avi.dullu@gmail.com`. It auto-adds the MX/TXT records; confirm once in Gmail. Now every "Request access" click lands in your inbox. (Free.)

That's the whole path: **upload → nameservers → custom domain → email routing.** ~15 minutes, all free tier.

### 217. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.259Z)

Yes — I see real PMF here, and it's worth locating precisely (I'll be honest, not hype):

**The PMF is in the *searchable rally index*, and conversational input is the right interface for it.** Your validated pain is "hours of footage nobody watches; hand-cutting is tedious." The wedge isn't "an auto highlight reel" (every AI clipper claims that) — it's **turning a match into a queryable index of rallies with the dead time stripped, then composing any reel on demand.** "Give me every rally over 10 seconds" is a killer demo because it's *self-serve editing without an editor*. Two honest caveats I'd bake into the page so we don't overpromise:
1. **The moat is the index, not the NL parser.** Natural language is cheap to build over a known schema; the durable value is rally segmentation quality (your actual hard-won asset). So I'll lead with "searchable rallies" and treat "just ask" as the delightful front door.
2. **Scope the queries to metadata you actually have today** — length, count, order, which game. "Rallies where X smashed" needs shot/player detection, which is your roadmap. I'll keep the page honest about that line.

## What changed (all live in your preview)
- **New headline:** *"Turn a full match into searchable rallies — and build any reel just by asking."*
- **Hero visual is now a conversational reel-builder:** a query bar (*"Give me a reel of every rally over 10 seconds"*) → the rally index timeline with the **6 matches lit green and the rest dimmed** → a composed **"Your reel · 6 rallies · 1:32 · dead time removed"** result bar. It tells the whole story in one glance.
- **New centerpiece section** ("The part people love → *Ask for the reel you want — in plain English*") with three example query→result cards, plus an **honest scope line**: today you filter by length / count / order / which game; shot-type and player queries are roadmap.
- **Reframed "what it does today"** cards: *Every rally indexed · Dead time removed · Ask in plain English · Stitched reel + summary*, and a new FAQ entry "What can I ask for?".

## My PMF read, as your cofounder
**Yes — and this is the sharpest framing of the product yet.** Three things make me believe it:
1. **It productizes your actual moat.** You already persist candidate rally segments — a *queryable index → stitch* surface is the natural product over the segmentation engine you've spent months sharpening. You're selling your strength, not a generic "AI clipper."
2. **The index is the wedge; language is the door.** The durable value is rally-detection quality (especially multi-court); NL is the friendly front-end that makes it self-serve for a non-technical coach. So: invest in the index, treat the chat as delight — and never let the copy promise a query the index can't answer yet.
3. **It strengthens your GTM loop.** "Ask for your reel" is a far better field-visit demo than "here's an auto reel," *and* every query result is a natural moment for a coach to say "that one's not a rally" — which is exactly the labelled-correction flywheel your whole strategy depends on.

The one discipline I'd hold: keep the alpha's queries to **length / count / order / game** (what the index actually knows) until shot/player detection lands. That keeps the magic honest.

This also tells you what to build next in the UI alpha: the **query → filter → stitch** surface over the rally index — the data's already there.

## Tonight stays simple
The go-live runbook is unchanged (GoDaddy → move nameservers to Cloudflare → Pages upload → custom domain → email routing). I've noted GoDaddy as your registrar and saved the positioning so we don't lose it. The `index.html` is ready to drag in.

1	# Video Recording Guidelines (DRAFT — thresholds pending empirical data)
2	
3	**Status:** 🚧 Draft / placeholder · **Date:** 2026-05-30
4	**Why this exists:** highlight quality is bounded by *input* quality. To get reliably good auto-generated highlights, the source video must be recorded a certain way (camera height, court position, resolution, frame rate, stability, framing). We want to (1) **derive** the minimum acceptable capture spec **empirically** from the precision/recall of the solutions we build, and (2) **publish user-facing best practices** so a user knows how to record footage that the product can index well.
5	
6	> **This is a tracked thought, not a finished standard.** The exact numeric thresholds below are **placeholders** to be replaced with values measured against our golden set. See "How we'll set the real numbers" (§3). Captured now (per the team's working agreement) so it isn't lost; to be revisited.
7	>
8	> **Update (2026-06-02):** the **frame-rate** and **camera-geometry** rows now carry **literature-backed starting values** (cited inline). They remain *starting points* to confirm empirically per §3. The golden-set **corpus** diversity spec (what footage to collect, not just how users should record) is now in [GOLDEN_SET_PHASE1_VIDEOS.md](GOLDEN_SET_PHASE1_VIDEOS.md).
9	>
10	> **Update (2026-06-07):** **AUDIO is now a first-class capture requirement** (new row below), per ADR-007 — we will use the shuttle "thwack" as a rally cue (audio+visual fusion lifts recall). This is a hard "record with audio on, mic unobstructed" ask, not a placeholder; efficacy on real GoPro audio is being validated (experiment E1 in [AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md](archives/research/AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md)).
11	
12	---
13	
14	## ⚠️ THE #1 CAPTURE RULE: ONE MATCH PER FRAME (single court)
15	
16	> **For the best auto-highlights, record a SINGLE match on a SINGLE court — with no other matches
17	> playing in the background or on adjacent courts.** This is the happy path the product is tuned for,
18	> and as of **2026-06-18 it is MEASURED, not a guess:**
19	>
20	> - On a golden **multi-court** clip, the rally detector fired **87 "rallies" against 43 real ones —
21	>   precision ≈ 0.20 (~80% false positives)**, almost all of them background-court games it mistook
22	>   for play.
23	> - On **single-court** clips, precision is markedly higher and rally boundaries are tight; local even
24	>   **out-recalls Gemini** on normal single-match play.
25	>
26	> **Multi-court is a KNOWN BOTTLENECK we are actively working to fix** (player-state + court-occupancy
27	> signals — see [RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md](RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md)). Until that lands,
28	> **single-match capture is strongly recommended** — a reel made from busy multi-court footage *will*
29	> include false "rallies" from the neighbouring games. **North star (for now): consistently beat Gemini
30	> on the single-match happy path.**
31	
32	---
33	
34	## 1. Why input capture matters (grounded in what we already know)
35	
36	ADR-004 found a concrete, capture-dependent failure mode: on **broadcast** footage the camera *tracks the players*, so player pixel-motion dips during rallies and spikes on pans/replays — making player-motion a poor rally proxy. The lesson generalizes: **the pipeline's accuracy is conditional on how the video was shot.** A **static, full-court** camera (typical of a fixed tripod recording) behaves very differently from broadcast — and is likely much friendlier to both the motion/detector stages and the shuttle-tracking substrate (WASB/TrackNet, ADR-001).
37	
38	So capture guidance isn't cosmetic — it directly moves precision/recall.
39	
40	---
41	
42	## 2. The dimensions that likely matter (hypotheses to test)
43	
44	| Dimension | Why it plausibly affects P/R | Placeholder guidance (UNVERIFIED) |
45	|---|---|---|
46	| **Resolution** | Shuttle is tiny & fast; too few pixels → detector misses it | ≥ **1080p** preferred; **720p** likely floor;...

| 6 | Cloudflare | (optional) **Analytics → Web Analytics** → copy token → paste into the snippet (or use automatic) |

The locality ladder (L0..L4) classifies designs by 'smallest artifact that must cross OUR network from the academy desktop', whereas BYO-storage+autosync is a different axis entirely — WHERE the canonical bytes live and HOW ingestion is triggered. The natural placement is a NEW dimension orthogonal to the ladder: a 'source backend / ingestion trigger' axis. Conceptually the user's-cloud-watched-folder is the AUTOMATED, networked cousin of L4 (sneakernet): same 'the original already lives somewhere we pull from, ~0 of OUR-network upload by the user' property, but the disk-in-a-bag is replaced by a watched Drive/S3/GCS folder and the 'a person' actor is replaced by a poll/webhook watcher. ### 422. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.270Z)

Grounded the assessment in the actual platform docs (quoting them, so this isn't me riffing) — and the verdict is clean:

## This is half-already-designed, on-thesis, and a real differentiator
The **"bring your own storage"** half is *already in your roadmap*. `PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md §3.1` literally specs a `StorageBackend` registry — **"Backends: local, gdrive, s3, gcs, azure … BYO-storage: the tenant registers their own bucket/Drive; raw video never leaves their account"** — built over `fsspec`/`smart_open`, with the auth posture already chosen (narrow `drive.file` OAuth, IAM AssumeRole + presigned URLs, *never persist long-lived keys*). It's roadmap item **P2**. And your own doc already makes the strategic case: *"none of Veo / Hudl / SwingVision / Spiideo offer true BYO-storage or BYO-compute — 'your video, your storage, your compute' is a genuine differentiator for privacy-sensitive academies."*

You've even **proven the ergonomics on yourselves**: `GOLDEN_DATA_SHARING.md` already runs a OneDrive folder that auto-syncs both ways between your GPU box and dev machine. That's exactly this pattern, in production, internally.

**What's genuinely new is the autosync trigger.** Across all the docs, there is *no* watcher/poller/webhook/"new file → process" component. The shipped async job model (#16) is **pull-on-request** (`POST /api/process → 202`). "ingestion" is even named as a layer but never built. So the work splits cleanly:

| Piece | Status |
|---|---|
| Connect GCS / Drive / OneDrive / S3 / Dropbox | **Designed** (`§3.1` StorageBackend, P2) — not built |
| `list()`/`stat()` primitives a poller needs | **Already in the ABC** |
| Dedup so re-syncs don't reprocess | **Already there** — `video_id` = content MD5 idempotency key |
| **Watch folder → auto-enqueue a job** | **Net-new** — the one real new component |
| Reels land back in the library | Existing flow |

## The honest risks — and the one decision that unblocks everything
**Compute placement is the fork that determines who pays and how private it really is:**
- **Desktop app pulls** (their machine watches their synced folder, processes locally): zero egress through us, no raw video ever touches our infra — the strongest privacy story. **But it's gated on your unsolved desktop P0 spike** (WASB is ~3.4× real-time even on a 2070S; a no-GPU laptop could be hours/clip or infeasible).
- **We pull (hosted):** simplest UX, but we briefly hold their video (which *weakens* the "never leaves your account" headline) and someone eats recurring egress. The clean middle is your own `§3.2` **"zero-infra BYO"** — storage stays in their bucket via signed URLs, compute on an ephemeral per-tenant GPU sandbox.

Pick that, and the rest falls out. The other live ones:
- **Egress / "surprise bill":** pulling 1–4 GB per match *out* of their cloud meters egress on *their* provider. Your cost story ("we don't store giant videos") holds for storage but **not automatically for egress** on a we-pull design. Locality discipline says pull a *proxy*, not the 10 GB original — which really wants a client (desktop).
- **OAuth reality:** the privacy-minimal `drive.file` scope only sees files *the app created*. Watching a user's *existing* footage folder needs `drive.readonly` (broader, higher-trust) + **Google/Microsoft/Dropbox app-verification/security review** + refresh-token custody. MS365 needs admin consent. That's a real weeks-long compliance gate, not a weekend.
- **"Stream it, never download" isn't fully true:** detection can stream a proxy, but **stitch reads the original**, and ffmpeg/cv2 want a local file — so originals (or ranged streams) come down at reel time. Be honest in the copy.
- **Autosync hygiene** (the new component's actual work): a watched folder is a firehose — in-flight partial uploads, non-video, HEVC/GoPro-chaptered clips, non-badminton footage, **and your own reels written back to the same bucket** (re-ingest loop). Needs a file-ready check, relevance/codec filtering, and the MD5 dedup ledger. Plus per-provider change-feeds all differ (GCS Pub/Sub, S3 events, Drive push channels that *expire*, Graph delta) — `fsspec` abstracts reads, not change events.
- **Consent gets *bigger*:** a background daemon that auto-processes "whatever lands" removes the human checkpoint → consent-at-capture, minors-exclusion, retention deadline, and "free tier never hits Gemini" become mandatory, not optional.

## Persona nuance worth locking
Your locality doc explicitly calls "expecting academies to manage S3/GCS buckets" a **non-starter**. So split it: **consumer cloud (Drive/OneDrive/Dropbox) → "watch this folder, reels appear"** for the non-technical academy/prosumer; **raw S3/GCS bucket BYO** for the technical/B2B tenant. Same engine, two front doors.

## My recommendation
**Strong yes as a direction — but it's a platform layer, not the next thing.** It sits *after* the immediate launch (tonight's page, the alpha web app, corpus growth) and rides on the **desktop P0 compute spike**, because the make-or-break question (where compute runs / who pays egress) is the same unknown that spike resolves. Beautifully, it composes with the new positioning: **autosync → index rallies → (coming) ask for your reel** = a zero-touch funnel.

When you're ready, I'd capture it as a tracked design doc (it spans three existing ones): connector half stays in `PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE §3.1` (P2), and I'd add a new **"IngestTrigger / Watcher"** seam there + push the egress/OAuth/retention open-questions into `VIDEO_LOCALITY_MODEL`'s ledger — it's effectively the autosync front-door to `STORAGE_SHARING_MODEL §7d` (your co-design item #15). I won't start it unprompted (owner-gated strategy), but say the word and I'll draft the doc with the compute-placement decision teed up as the lead question.

For tonight, though — this changes nothing about go-live. Page's ready when you are. 🚀

### 443. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.271Z)

Perfect timing. One thing to flag so we don't stall: the **two nameserver values you'll paste into GoDaddy come *from* Cloudflare** — so we grab those first, then drop them into the GoDaddy screen you're on. Keep that GoDaddy tab open.

## Step 1 — Cloudflare: add the domain, get the nameservers (~3 min)
1. New tab → **dash.cloudflare.com** → log in, or **Sign up** (free, ~1 min) if you don't have an account.
2. Click **Add a domain** (a.k.a. "Add site") → type `khelsutra.guru` → **Continue**.
3. Choose the **Free** plan → **Continue**.
4. Cloudflare scans existing DNS (there's basically nothing — it's a parked domain) → **Continue**.
5. You'll land on a screen titled something like **"Update your nameservers."** It shows **2 values** ending in `.ns.cloudflare.com` (e.g. `aria.ns.cloudflare.com` and `rob.ns.cloudflare.com`).

**→ Tell me those two values (or just keep them on screen).** Those are what go into GoDaddy.

## Step 2 — GoDaddy: swap the nameservers (the one GoDaddy step)
On your GoDaddy tab:
1. **Domain Portfolio** (or **My Products**) → click **khelsutra.guru**.
2. Find **Nameservers** → **Change** (might be under a "DNS" or "Manage DNS" section).
3. Choose **"I'll use my own nameservers"** / **"Enter my own nameservers"**.
4. Replace the existing entries with Cloudflare's **two** values → **Save**.
5. If GoDaddy warns that this moves DNS management away from GoDaddy — that's expected, **confirm it**.

### 456. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.272Z)

Two things, and the first is **good news**:

✅ **The GoDaddy message means you're done at GoDaddy** — it's confirming your nameservers propagated and Cloudflare now manages DNS. Don't touch GoDaddy again.

The Pages error is just **leftover GoDaddy "parking" records** that Cloudflare auto-imported when you added the domain. There's an `A`/`CNAME` record sitting on `khelsutra.guru` (pointing at GoDaddy's parking page), and Pages won't overwrite it. Delete those and retry — 2 minutes.

## Fix: clear the parking records, then re-add the domain
1. Cloudflare dash → click the **`khelsutra.guru`** domain → left sidebar **DNS → Records**.
2. You'll see a few auto-imported records. **Delete** these:
   - The **`A`** record with **Name `khelsutra.guru`** (or `@`) — that's the parking IP.
   - Any **`A`/`CNAME`** with **Name `www`** — parking too.
   - (Safe to also delete any stray parking `CNAME`/`TXT`. **Leave** the `NS` records and a `_domainconnect` CNAME if present — those are harmless.)
   - Since this is a freshly-parked domain, **all existing records are GoDaddy junk** — nothing of yours to lose.
3. Go back to **Workers & Pages → `khelsutra-site` → Custom domains → Set up a domain** → enter `khelsutra.guru` again. It'll now succeed and **auto-create the correct record** (proxied) + start provisioning SSL.
4. Repeat for **`www.khelsutra.guru`** (or set a redirect — your call).

After you delete the A record, the apex briefly has no record — that's expected; Pages recreates the right one. SSL can take a few minutes to go green.

### 487. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.274Z)

Checked the live cert and redirect behavior. **The encryption itself is solid — but there's one real gap a security scanner *will* flag.** Here's the honest picture:

**✅ Certificate is correct and trusted:**
- Issued by **Google Trust Services** (Cloudflare's Universal SSL CA) — publicly trusted, no warnings.
- Covers **`khelsutra.guru` *and* `*.khelsutra.guru`** (SAN), so `www` is covered by the wildcard too (and we already saw `www` serve over HTTPS fine).
- Valid **Jun 20 → Sep 18 2026**, auto-renewing. HTTPS to the site validates cleanly (no chain errors).

**⚠️ The thing that gets flagged — no forced HTTPS:**
- `http://khelsutra.guru` currently returns **200 over plain HTTP** — it does **not** redirect to HTTPS. So the page is reachable insecurely, and browsers/scanners will flag "available over HTTP / not secure."
- There's also **no HSTS header**, which security audits commonly flag.

**Fix — 2 toggles in Cloudflare (1 minute), no redeploy:**
Cloudflare dash → **`khelsutra.guru`** → **SSL/TLS → Edge Certificates**:
1. **"Always Use HTTPS" → ON.** This makes `http://` 301-redirect to `https://`. *This is the one that closes the main flag.*
2. **"HTTP Strict Transport Security (HSTS)" → Enable** → max-age ~6 months. (One caveat: HSTS is sticky — once set, browsers force HTTPS for the domain — but for a static site that's always on HTTPS, it's exactly what you want.)
3. *(Optional hygiene)* same page → **"Minimum TLS Version" → 1.2**.

Also worth a glance: **SSL/TLS → Overview → encryption mode** should read **Full (strict)** — though for a Workers/Pages deployment the origin *is* Cloudflare, so this is largely automatic; just make sure it's **not** set to "Flexible."

Net: your data-in-transit is genuinely protected (valid TLS cert), but flip **"Always Use HTTPS" ON** so nothing's reachable over plain HTTP — that's the toggle that keeps a security scanner from marking it "unprotected." Want me to re-verify the http→https redirect once you've flipped it?

Sure — here's the full picture on the **email redirect** (Cloudflare calls it **Email Routing**), which is what makes your `hello@khelsutra.guru` "Request access" button actually work.

## What it is
It's **free email forwarding**. You don't get a new inbox to log into — instead, anything sent to `hello@khelsutra.guru` is **automatically forwarded to an inbox you already use** (your Gmail). So when someone clicks "Request access" on the site, the email lands in your normal Gmail, and you reply from there.

- It's **receive-only forwarding** — think of it as a mail-forwarding sticker on a PO box, not a new mailbox.
- It's **free**, runs entirely on Cloudflare (works because your domain's DNS is now on Cloudflare).
- You can have many addresses: `hello@`, `avi@`, `support@` — all forwarding wherever you want.

## How to set it up (~2 min, no code)
Cloudflare dashboard → click the **`khelsutra.guru`** domain → left sidebar **Email → Email Routing** → **Get started / Enable**, then:
1. **Add a destination address** = your Gmail (e.g. `avi.dullu@gmail.com`). Cloudflare sends a **verification email** to that Gmail — open it and click the confirm link. (One-time; proves you own the inbox.)
2. **Create a custom address**: `hello` @ `khelsutra.guru` → **Action: Send to** → pick your verified Gmail → **Save**.
3. *(Optional)* Toggle **Catch-all** so *anything*@khelsutra.guru (typos, future names) also forwards to you.

Cloudflare then **auto-adds the DNS records** it needs — three **MX** records (`*.mx.cloudflare.net`) and an **SPF TXT** record. You don't touch those; it does it for you. Since khelsutra.guru is a fresh domain with no existing email, there's nothing to conflict with.

That's it — test it by emailing `hello@khelsutra.guru` from any account; it should appear in your Gmail within seconds.

## The one honest caveat: receiving ≠ sending
Email Routing **forwards inbound mail only.** When you reply, by default it goes **from your Gmail address**, not from `hello@khelsutra.guru`. For an invite-only alpha that's totally fine — you'll just reply personally.

If later you want replies to **appear as `hello@khelsutra.guru`**, that's a separate step (Gmail "Send mail as" needs an outbound SMTP relay, which Cloudflare Routing doesn't provide — you'd add a small sender service like a transactional email provider). Not worth it now; flag it for beta.

## Why it matters right now
Your landing page's button is `mailto:hello@khelsutra.guru?subject=KhelSutra alpha access…`. Until Email Routing is set up, **that address doesn't exist** and those emails bounce. Once you do the 3 steps above, every "Request access" click quietly lands in your inbox. ✅

Want me to verify it's working once you've set it up? I can send a quick check or confirm the MX/SPF records resolved correctly.

- **Do you have a GPU machine?** (Yes / Not sure / No / prefer cloud) — the **compute-placement** signal.
- **Where do your recordings live?** (Drive · OneDrive · Dropbox · S3/GCS · SD/local · Not sure) — the **storage-backend** signal.
- Free-text "Anything else?" + consent + the minors/privacy note. Honeypot keeps bots out.

I verified: renders cleanly, the academy-toggle works, all 14 fields present, **zero console errors**. Those two questions aren't just lead-gen — they're literally the BYO-compute / BYO-storage forks from the platform analysis, so early answers tell you *what to build next*.

## The diligence layer (as you asked)
- **`docs/REQUEST_ACCESS_FORM.md`** — tracked design doc: decisions-locked (Worker + D1), form spec, D1 schema, API contract, privacy, the deploy-model change, a §6 progress tracker, and a definition-of-done.
- **`NEXT_STEPS.md`** — the repo's live what's-next.

## Two honest notes
1. **Not live until you publish.** No git auto-deploy, so the form goes live when you **re-upload `khelsutra/site/`** to Cloudflare (that folder is the source of truth now).
2. **Until the Worker ships, the form uses a `mailto` fallback** — submissions open a prefilled email rather than hitting a database. That's by design (P1 ships value today; P2 wires real collection).

## Your call on two things
- **Merge #2 now, or review the design doc + form first?** (I validated it; it's a low-risk static change — but it's your repo and the doc locks decisions, so your call.)
- **Proceed to P2 — the Worker + D1 backend?** I'd write `wrangler.toml` + the Worker (`/api/request-access` → D1) + `schema.sql` as the next PR, and hand you the exact `wrangler d1 create` / `deploy` commands (those run under your Cloudflare account). ### 619. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.281Z)

🎉 **That's the real proof** — your sister clicking "Request access" and it landing in your inbox confirms the whole **Email Routing loop works end-to-end** (the live `mailto` CTA → `hello@khelsutra.guru` → your Gmail). That's the one thing DNS couldn't prove for me earlier. Closed. ✅

## Should you link GitHub ↔ Cloudflare? **Yes — it's both quicker *and* safer.**
The wins are exactly what you'd want:
- **Quick:** `git push` / merge → Cloudflare builds + deploys automatically. No more manual drag-upload.
- **Safe — three ways:**
  - **Versioned rollback** — every deploy is tied to a commit; one click to roll back to any previous deployment.
  - **PR preview URLs** — each PR gets its own live preview link, so you (and I) can *see* a change rendered before it touches production. That's a real upgrade to the review discipline you asked for.
  - **No drift** — the repo becomes the single source of truth; "what's in git" == "what's live." (Literally the trap we just hit when I copied to the wrong folder — git-deploy eliminates it.)

## The timing is perfect — pair it with the Worker (P2)
Here's the one nuance: Cloudflare deploys a Worker from a **`wrangler.toml`** in the repo (via "Workers Builds"). We're *about* to add that anyway for the form's D1 backend. So the clean sequence is:

1. **I build P2** — `wrangler.toml` + the Worker (`/api/request-access` → D1) + `schema.sql`, in a PR. This makes the repo "connect-ready."
2. **You connect the repo** in Cloudflare (Workers Builds → pick `avidullu/khelsutra`, root dir `site/`, bind D1). One-time, ~5 min — I'll give exact click-by-click steps.
3. From then on: **merge to `master` → auto-deploys to khelsutra.guru; PRs → preview URLs.**

**One behavior change to know (it's the "safe" part):** production only moves when you merge to `master`. Feature branches/PRs deploy to *preview*, not production — so nothing goes live by accident.

A couple of setup details we'll handle during the connect: re-attaching the `khelsutra.guru` custom domain to the git-deployed Worker, and the D1 binding. All straightforward; I'll walk it.

## So, where this leaves us
- **PR #2 (the form)** is still awaiting your merge call. Once git is connected, **merging it = it deploys automatically.** (Until then, you'd re-upload `site/` to publish — but I'd just wait for the git connection so the form ships cleanly.)
- **Recommendation:** let me **build P2 now** (so the repo is connect-ready), then you do the one-time Cloudflare connect, and from that point everything is push-to-deploy with previews + rollback.

## What
- Converts `site/` into a **Cloudflare Worker with static assets**:
  - `src/index.js` — serves `public/` and handles **`POST /api/request-access`** → validate + honeypot-filter + **insert into Cloudflare D1** (`access_requests`).

- **Privacy:** stores only submitted fields + coarse `cf-ipcountry` (no raw IP); consent flag; contact-only.

# Cloudflare Workers Free Plan Limits

Based on the pricing documentation, here are the specific free-tier limitations:

**Requests:** The free plan permits "100,000 per day" of inbound requests to your Worker.

**CPU Time:** You receive "10 milliseconds of CPU time per invocation" at no charge, with no duration limit otherwise.

**Static Assets:** Excellent news for static content—"Requests to static assets are free and unlimited," meaning they don't count against your request quota and incur no charges.

Additionally, the free plan includes other complimentary services like Workers Logs (200,000 log events daily with 3-day retention), Workers KV access, and Hyperdrive database queries (100,000 per day).
[/external_agent_tool_result]

### 681. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.285Z)

Confirmed from Cloudflare's pricing docs — **no, this stays comfortably in the free tier**, by a wide margin. And critically: on the free plan, exceeding a limit returns errors (rate-limits you), it **does not auto-bill** — so there's no surprise-charge risk, and no card needed.

Here's the math against your actual (invite-only alpha) usage:

| Component | Free-tier limit | Your usage | Headroom |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Workers — static serving** | **Free & unlimited** (doesn't count) | All landing-page traffic | ∞ |
| **Workers — dynamic requests** | 100,000 / **day** | 1 per form submit → dozens? | ~99.9% unused |
| **D1 — rows written** | 100,000 / **day** | 1 per submit | microscopic |
| **D1 — rows read** | 5,000,000 / **day** | your occasional `SELECT` | microscopic |
| **D1 — storage** | 5 GB | ~1 KB/row → ~5M submissions to fill | never |
| **Email Routing · SSL · DNS · Analytics** | Free | — | — |

The key insight from the docs: **serving the site is free and uncounted** — only the *form POSTs* hit the Worker, and you'd need **100,000 submissions in a single day** to touch the limit. Git-connected builds also have a generous free monthly allotment, far above your commit cadence.

**So you'd only ever cross into paid if you deliberately opt into the Workers Paid plan ($5/mo)** — which you'd do for *scale* (millions of requests) or extras (Durable Objects, higher CPU), none of which the landing page + alpha form needs.

**One honest framing, so the cost picture is clear:** the real money in this product lives in **video processing** — GPU compute, the Gemini API, and video storage/egress (the stuff we cost-analyzed in the engine repo). That's a separate system. **The marketing site + lead form is, and will stay, free.** The BYO-storage/compute direction those form answers feed into is precisely the lever that keeps the *expensive* part cheap too.

### 900. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.300Z)

**Verified, diagnosed, fixed, and now in sync** ✅ — both serve **md5 `1598a01a9810`**, byte-identical.

**What differed:** *line endings only* — the droplet had **CRLF**, Cloudflare had **LF**. Identical content; they render the same. **Root cause:** the droplet was `scp`'d from the Windows working copy (git's `autocrlf` makes it CRLF), while Cloudflare deploys straight from git (LF). I normalized the droplet to LF → they now match exactly.

**Why the tests didn't catch it:** the harness boots `server.js` and checks the form *works* on each host — it tests **code/behavior**, not **byte-parity between the two live deployments**. And CRLF vs LF changes neither rendering nor the `id="af"` check, so a content test passes for both. The drift was a *transport* artifact (scp from Windows), invisible to a code test. Fix = make line endings deterministic + add a live-parity check. Doing both now:

=== _allowed_hosts_from_env: REPLACE or append the loopback default? ===
def _allowed_hosts_from_env(env: Optional[Dict[str, str]] = None) -> List[str]:
    """P0c: Host-header allowlist — a DNS-rebinding guard for the localhost appliance. A malicious
    web page can point a domain it controls at 127.0.0.1 and script requests to the local server;
    the browser sends ``Host: attacker.com``, which this rejects (the server only answers loopback
    Host headers by default). ``RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS`` (comma-separated) overrides; the default keeps
    Starlette's TestClient host ``testserver`` so the in-process suite isn't broken. Read from the
    ENV (not config.json) so importing ``backend.main`` does zero filesystem I/O; a routable/edge
    deploy sets its public host (or ``*`` when fronted by an auth gate that validates the host)."""
    src = os.environ if env is None else env
    raw = (src.get("RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS", "") or "").strip()
    hosts = [h.strip() for h in raw.split(",") if h.strip()]
    # IPv4 loopback (the default bind) + the TestClient host. NB: Starlette's TrustedHostMiddleware
    # matches on host.split(":")[0], which mangles a bracketed IPv6 Host ("[::1]:port"), so IPv6
    # loopback is NOT supported by this guard — access the appliance via 127.0.0.1/localhost.
    return hosts or ["localhost", "127.0.0.1", "testserver"]


# DNS-rebinding guard. Bound to localhost by default, so only loopback Host headers are honoured
# (a remote page can't read responses cross-origin AND its Host header is rejected here). www_redirect
# off so an unexpected ``www.`` host is rejected rather than 307-redirected.
[/external_agent_tool_result]
